PRG bank 6

not yet established · $8000-$BFFF

Documented
100.00%
Bytes
16,384 / 16,384
Regions
885
File offsets
0x18010-0x1C00F

Why this bank sits where it does

Opens with a 7-entry JMP table at $8000-$8014. Nothing else in this bank has been traced.

An address on this page does not identify a byte. $8000-$BFFF is the switchable window, so every address below exists in seven banks at once and only bank 6 has these particular bytes at it. Any address taken from a debugger, a wiki or another disassembly has to carry a bank with it before it means anything — tools/whereis.py --bytes settles which bank really holds a given sequence.

Layout

codedatalevelpaddingpointer_tableunclaimed

Unclaimed

None - every byte in this span is claimed by a region.

Far-call argument at $8027 data confirmed

bank6.inline_8027 · file 0x18037-0x18039 · 3 bytes · PRG bank 6 · CPU $8027-$8029

Three bytes of far-call argument following `JSR $D76F` at bank 6 $8024 - bank number, then the target address minus one, little-endian. They select **bank 0 $8000**. The stored word is $7FFF rather than $8000 because $D790 pushes these two bytes as a fake return address and lets RTS add the one back; that is not an inference, it is what the four instructions at $D799-$D7A4 do. A far call is the one reference in this ROM that no grep for an operand will ever surface, so the target is recorded here where a reader will find it.

$8027  00 FF 7F                                         |...|

TGL RUNNING SCORE (slot `$8012`): add the stage's points code confirmed

bank6.code_805A · file 0x1806A-0x18080 · 23 bytes · PRG bank 6 · CPU $805A-$8070

Called by bank 3 `$80D3` (the TGL reward screen). Six decimal digits: `$04B3-$04B8` (the running total, most significant first) += `$04B9-$04BE` (the points gained in the corridor, from `$04B9`...), digit by digit from the last with a carry chain (a digit of 10 or more has 10 subtracted and carries). A carry out of the top digit skips to `$8093` (255). RUN: 300 random digit sets: the stored digits equal the decimal sum.

$805A  A0 05 18 B9 B9 04 79 B3 04 C9 0A 90 02 E9 0A 99  |......y.........|
$806A  B3 04 88 10 EE B0 22                             |......"|

Object dispatch, low bytes (types 1-126) pointer_table confirmed

bank6.tbl_80DF · file 0x180EF-0x1816C · 126 bytes · PRG bank 6 · CPU $80DF-$815C

The low half of bank 6's split object-behaviour table: $821E does LDA $815D,Y / PHA / LDA $80DE,Y / PHA / RTS with Y = type AND $7F. Type 0 is filtered by BEQ at $8213, so $80DE - low[0] - is never read, and it is in fact the last byte of the traced code before it (the same overlap the metasprite table uses). Types 1-126 are here; type 127's low byte is $815D, the first byte of the high half, which is never read as high[0]. So this is 126 bytes between code and the high half. CORRECTION: CLAUDE.md said find-tables.py claimed both halves; it only ever claimed $815D - a reader-bounded table cannot bound the half that no later read follows.

GAME OVER code confirmed

bank6.code_8247 · file 0x18257-0x18279 · 35 bytes · PRG bank 6 · CPU $8247-$8269

Slot 0 := `$84`, objects 4 to 1 removed (`$CCBF`), `JSR $C0A2` (`$CD99`), sounds `$1F` and `$37`, `$A0` bit 0 cleared, then a far call (inline `00 0E 80`) to bank 0 `$800F` (-> the GAME OVER screen, CONTINUE and the restart). RUN: life and shown 0 gave type `$84`, slots 1-4 empty, `$A0` bit 0 clear; either non-zero gave `$81`. The sounds are read from the code, not run.

$8247  A9 84 8D 00 05 A2 04 20 48 C0 CA D0 FA 20 A2 C0  |....... H.... ..|
$8257  A9 1F 20 30 C0 A9 37 20 30 C0 A5 A0 29 FE 85 A0  |.. 0..7 0...)...|
$8267  20 06 C0                                         | ..|

Far-call argument at $826A data confirmed

bank6.inline_826A · file 0x1827A-0x1827C · 3 bytes · PRG bank 6 · CPU $826A-$826C

Three bytes of far-call argument following `JSR $D76F` at bank 6 $8267 - bank number, then the target address minus one, little-endian. They select **bank 0 $800F**. The stored word is $800E rather than $800F because $D790 pushes these two bytes as a fake return address and lets RTS add the one back; that is not an inference, it is what the four instructions at $D799-$D7A4 do. A far call is the one reference in this ROM that no grep for an operand will ever surface, so the target is recorded here where a reader will find it.

$826A  00 0E 80                                         |...|

Nothing is stored yet. RUN: 1,500 random states (random class maps, positions and velocities) on the model equalled an independent model of this reading in every output.

Move: the class of the target cell (`$6C`) code confirmed

bank6.code_82CE · file 0x182DE-0x18301 · 36 bytes · PRG bank 6 · CPU $82CE-$82F1

The class map `$0400-$045F` is 8 bytes a cell row, two cells a byte: index = (probe y AND `$F0`) / 2 + new column / 2, the high nibble for an even column and the low for an odd one. An index of `$60` or more (below the 12 rows of the room) reads as class 9. `$6C` := the class of (new row, new column): the diagonal cell. (The same lookup is repeated three times in this routine.)

$82CE  A5 10 4A 85 10 A5 11 4A 4A 4A 4A 85 11 4A 05 10  |..J....JJJJ..J..|
$82DE  A8 B9 00 04 B0 04 4A 4A 4A 4A 29 0F C0 60 90 02  |......JJJJ)..`..|
$82EE  A9 09 85 6C                                      |...l|

Move: `$12` - the x-step cell is NOT consulted code confirmed

bank6.code_8355 · file 0x18365-0x1836B · 7 bytes · PRG bank 6 · CPU $8355-$835B

The code does `LDA $6E` and then `LDA $8425,Y` with Y still holding `$6D`: there is no `TAY`, so the loaded `$6E` is thrown away and `$12` := `$8425[$6D]`, a copy of `$11`. It looks like a missed instruction. **Effect, measured:** a model that reads `$8425[$6E]` here disagreed with the real routine in 9 of 1,500 random states; the model that copies `$11` agreed in all of them. So a move where only the x-step cell blocks is judged as if it were free in x.

$8355  A5 6E B9 25 84 85 12                             |.n.%...|

bank6.tbl_8701 · file 0x18711-0x18731 · 33 bytes · PRG bank 6 · CPU $8701-$8721

$86AF computes Y = $3D x 11 + $3C - the selected weapon's level times eleven plus the weapon - and $86C1-$86DC read four tables with it: LDA $86F5,Y, $8716,Y, $8737,Y and $8758,Y. A weapon object exists only at level 1-3 and for weapons 1-11, so Y runs 12-44 and each table's bytes are its base + 12 to base + 44: 33 entries, 11 weapons x 3 levels. The read BASES are 12 bytes before the tables - the overlap trick again; at $86F5 those 12 bytes are the tail of the weapon jump table. CORRECTION: tools/find-tables.py claimed the four as 33-byte tables at the read bases, which put the first on top of the jump table. This one ($86F5 base): a non-zero entry replaces the object's $05A8, its attack.

Table, 11 bytes data confirmed

bank6.tbl_8784 · file 0x18794-0x1879E · 11 bytes · PRG bank 6 · CPU $8784-$878E

A table of 11 bytes at $8784, read by LDA aby at $86A9. THE EXTENT COMES FROM THE CODE, not from the data: the same routine reads another table based at $878F from $86A3, 6 bytes away in the instruction stream, so this table runs exactly up to that base. Two bases read by unrelated code would bound nothing, which is why the reads have to be neighbours. **WHAT THE BYTES MEAN IS NOT ESTABLISHED** - this region claims the extent and the reader and stops there. Generated by tools/find-tables.py.

$8784  34 1A 21 21 1A 37 1A 1A 1A 00 00                 |4.!!.7.....|

Weapon 9: when does it hurt? code confirmed

bank6.code_8AC9 · file 0x18AD9-0x18AED · 21 bytes · PRG bank 6 · CPU $8AC9-$8ADD

`$0578,X` := 8 (no collision with ordinary enemies, whose mask is `$30`); then the level's frame mask `$8AE6[level]` AND the frame counter `$41`: non-zero leaves it at 8, zero sets `$8AE9[level]` = `$18`, `$38`, `$3C`. So level 1 hurts on every other frame (`$41` bit 0 clear), levels 2 and 3 on every frame with wider masks. RUN: level 1 flags 18 08 18 08 for `$41` = 0..3; levels 2 and 3 `38` / `3C` throughout. The `RTS` at `$8ADD` is also the byte `$60` that the tables' bases count from.

$8AC9  A9 08 9D 78 05 A4 3D B9 E6 8A 25 41 D0 06 B9 E9  |...x..=...%A....|

Far-call argument at $97AE data confirmed

bank6.inline_97AE · file 0x197BE-0x197C0 · 3 bytes · PRG bank 6 · CPU $97AE-$97B0

Three bytes of far-call argument following `JSR $D76F` at bank 6 $97AB - bank number, then the target address minus one, little-endian. They select **bank 1 $8009**. The stored word is $8008 rather than $8009 because $D790 pushes these two bytes as a fake return address and lets RTS add the one back; that is not an inference, it is what the four instructions at $D799-$D7A4 do. A far call is the one reference in this ROM that no grep for an operand will ever surface, so the target is recorded here where a reader will find it.

$97AE  01 08 80                                         |...|

Far-call argument at $99F4 data confirmed

bank6.inline_99F4 · file 0x19A04-0x19A06 · 3 bytes · PRG bank 6 · CPU $99F4-$99F6

Three bytes of far-call argument following `JSR $D76F` at bank 6 $99F1 - bank number, then the target address minus one, little-endian. They select **bank 2 $800F**. The stored word is $800E rather than $800F because $D790 pushes these two bytes as a fake return address and lets RTS add the one back; that is not an inference, it is what the four instructions at $D799-$D7A4 do. A far call is the one reference in this ROM that no grep for an operand will ever surface, so the target is recorded here where a reader will find it.

$99F4  02 0E 80                                         |...|

Bank 6 identity byte data confirmed

bank6.bank_id_BFFF · file 0x1C00F-0x1C00F · 1 bytes · PRG bank 6 · CPU $BFFF-$BFFF

One byte at $BFFF, the last byte of PRG bank 6, holding the bank's own number. Banks 0 to 6 each carry one; bank 7 does not, because bank 7's last byte is at $FFFF and belongs to the IRQ vector. These seven bytes are the ROM half of a boot-time mapper self-test. $FF23-$FF43, reached from the reset handler, is a loop over Y = 6 down to 0: TYA / JSR $D705 selects the bank in Y, CPY $BFFF then compares Y against the byte this region holds, and BEQ $FF41 falls through to DEY only when the two agree. If they do not, LDA $FF3A,Y fetches a per-bank code, stores it at $0180, calls $DED2 and then spins forever at JMP $FF37. So the game refuses to start unless every one of the seven switchable banks can be selected and answers with its own number - which is the check a UxROM board needs, because a bus conflict on the latch fails silently and leaves the wrong bank mapped. Reading the byte at $BFFF works no matter which bank is live precisely because every bank has one at the same address and they all differ. NOT ESTABLISHED: what $DED2 does with $0180, and therefore whether the failure is displayed or merely hangs.

$BFFF  06                                               |.|
